CLIENT EDITION / PRODUCTION PATTERNS




Agentic Code
Fieldbook 1000
1,000 compact patterns for building, testing, and operating AI agents

25 TOPICS     x   10 CORE PATTERNS      x   4 APPLIED CONTEXTS


Provider-neutral Python reference fragments | Searchable | Client-ready


Design language inspired by the supplied dark IDE reference.




                                                                          0001
START HERE / ORGANIZATION



1,000 patterns, one operating model
The catalog is broad on purpose, but every entry follows the same production discipline.

25 TOPICS              From agent loops and tools through memory, RAG, safety, evals, durable
                       workflows, APIs, streaming, and operations.

10 PATTERNS            Each topic is reduced to ten reusable techniques so you can learn concepts before
                       framework syntax.

4 CONTEXTS             Every technique is applied to support, research, commerce, and operations to
                       show how the same guard changes with the job.

1000 TOTAL             Each pattern is numbered, searchable, and paired with when-to-use and
                       production-watch guidance.




                                                                                                      0002
START HERE / PATTERN ANATOMY



Read each snippet in four passes
The code is intentionally compact. The educational value comes from seeing the guard, effect,
and failure path together.

1 / INTENT             Start with the title and intent line. Identify what invariant the snippet is protecting.

2 / CODE               Read the guard first, then the effect, then the return or failure branch. Preserve
                       that order when adapting it.

3 / USE IT             Use the pattern only where its trigger actually exists; do not add retries, memory,
                       or multi-agent work by reflex.

4 / WATCH              Treat the warning as a test requirement. Your implementation is incomplete until
                       that failure mode is covered.




                                                                                                            0003
START HERE / WORKFLOW



Use any snippet in six steps
Adapt the pattern to your stack without turning a small safety mechanism into a framework
rewrite.

STEP 1                  Name the real invariant: maximum steps, allowed tools, schema, timeout,
                        approval, idempotency, or another hard boundary.

STEP 2                  Map model, tools, store, policy, and trace calls to thin adapters around your SDKs
                        and infrastructure.

STEP 3                  Copy the smallest pattern that enforces the invariant; keep state changes and
                        side effects explicit.

STEP 4                  Add the negative path first: timeout, denial, duplicate, malformed output,
                        cancellation, stale state, or dependency failure.

STEP 5                  Write a deterministic test that proves both the happy path and the failure path
                        before connecting a live model.

STEP 6                  Add telemetry, set a rollout flag, canary it, evaluate outcomes, and keep a fast
                                                                                                         0004
                        rollback path.
START HERE / ADAPTER CONTRACT



Keep the snippets provider-neutral
Map these five seams to your model SDK, tools, state store, policy engine, and telemetry stack.


  REFERENCE CONTRACT

  class Runtime:
      model: ModelAdapter                  # complete(), stream(), next_action()
      tools: ToolRegistry                  # call(), call_async(), preview()
      store: StateStore                    # get(), put(), compare_and_swap()
      policy: PolicyEngine                 # allowed(), evaluate()
      trace: Telemetry                     # event(), span(), metric()

  runtime = Runtime(...)
  result = runtime.model.complete("Goal: solve the user task")


Snippets are intentionally small fragments: keep the guard, adapt the adapter names, then test the failure path.



                                                                                                         0005
FOUNDATION / RELIABILITY



Reliability comes before cleverness
A production agent should have multiple independent brakes so one bad model decision cannot
consume the entire system.

BOUNDS                 Set hard ceilings for steps, wall-clock time, retries, concurrency, queue depth,
                       tokens, and cost.

FAILURES               Classify errors as validation, permission, transient dependency, timeout,
                       cancellation, or internal - then handle each differently.

EFFECTS                Make side effects idempotent, record dedupe keys, and assume retries and
                       duplicate deliveries will eventually happen.

RECOVERY               Checkpoint resumable state, use durable orchestration for long waits, and make
                       fallback or human review explicit states.




                                                                                                          0006
FOUNDATION / TOOL SAFETY



The model proposes; code authorizes
Tool calling is safe only when the executable boundary remains deterministic and outside
model control.

SCHEMA                 Reject unknown fields, enforce types, enums, bounds, nesting, and schema
                       versions before dispatch.

PERMISSION             Derive allowed tools and actions from the authenticated actor, tenant, policy, and
                       current workflow state.

PREVIEW                Preview high-impact actions and bind approval to the exact action, arguments,
                       actor, and expiration.

RESULT                 Normalize tool output and failures before feeding them back into the agent loop;
                       redact sensitive fields before telemetry.




                                                                                                       0007
FOUNDATION / STATE + MEMORY



State is required; memory is optional
State answers what the current run must know. Memory answers what a future run is allowed
to remember.

STATE                  Checkpoint goals, steps, facts, tool outcomes, status, and version so a run can
                       resume without repeating effects.

CONCURRENCY            Use tenant-scoped keys and compare-and-swap, locks, or leases when multiple
                       workers can touch the same run.

MEMORY                 Store only durable value with provenance, consent, TTLs, dedupe, tenant isolation,
                       and an explicit delete path.

SEPARATION             Do not persist private scratch data by default; keep transient reasoning aids
                       separate from user-visible or durable state.




                                                                                                         0008
FOUNDATION / EVIDENCE



Retrieval needs an evidence contract
RAG is not just search plus a prompt. It is a chain of filters that must preserve what evidence
supported the answer.

RETRIEVE                Rewrite carefully, combine retrieval signals where useful, apply tenant metadata
                        filters, and cap top-k.

RANK                    Diversify and rerank candidates so context is relevant without repeating
                        near-duplicate chunks.

PACK                    Fit evidence to a deterministic context budget and preserve source IDs through
                        every ranking stage.

ABSTAIN                 When evidence is weak, return needs-evidence or human-review instead of
                        manufacturing certainty.




                                                                                                         0009
FOUNDATION / HUMAN CONTROL



Approval must bind to the exact action
A human review screen is only meaningful when the eventual effect is the same effect the
human approved.

BIND                   Bind approval to actor, action, normalized arguments or payload hash, resource,
                       tenant, and expiration.

PREVIEW                Show the proposed change and warnings before approval; require explicit
                       confirmation for irreversible actions.

EXPIRE                 Do not let old approvals authorize replanned work. Expire or invalidate approvals
                       when material inputs change.

AUDIT                  Write an immutable approval receipt and link it to the side effect's request or
                       idempotency key.




                                                                                                         0010
FOUNDATION / TESTING + EVALS



Test mechanisms; evaluate behavior
Unit tests and agent evaluations answer different questions, and a production release needs
both.

TEST                   Use fake models and tools to deterministically force malformed output, timeouts,
                       retries, cancellations, duplicates, and policy denials.

EVALUATE               Run frozen golden cases for quality, tool trajectory, safety, latency, and cost using
                       versioned evaluators.

REGRESS                Compare candidate behavior against a baseline and define release thresholds
                       before looking at the result.

CANARY                 Roll out to a small deterministic cohort, monitor error-budget burn, and freeze or
                       roll back automatically when necessary.




                                                                                                         0011
FOUNDATION / OBSERVABILITY



Trace decisions without tracing secrets
A useful trace explains what the agent attempted and why a boundary fired, while keeping
sensitive payloads out of logs and metrics.

IDENTITY               Propagate request, tenant, actor, agent, prompt-version, and state-version
                       identifiers through model and tool spans.

METRICS                Measure latency, token use, cost, tool failures, retries, queue depth, approval
                       rates, and completion outcomes.

REDACTION              Redact sensitive fields before serialization; never put secrets or high-cardinality
                       user data into metric labels.

REPLAY                 Record enough stable metadata to reproduce the decision path with fakes,
                       without replaying real side effects.




                                                                                                             0012
FOUNDATION / ROLLOUT



Production starts with a rollback path
Treat the agent as a continuously evaluated distributed system, not a prompt that happens to
be served over HTTP.

CONFIG                 Validate environment configuration at startup and keep operational limits typed
                       and versioned.

HEALTH                 Separate readiness from liveness; a process can be alive while its required
                       dependencies make it unready.

DRAIN                  Stop accepting new work, let bounded in-flight work finish, then requeue owned
                       jobs before shutdown.

ROLLBACK               Ship behind a feature flag or deterministic canary route with an immediate switch
                       back to the last known-good behavior.




                                                                                                         0013
LEARNING PATH / 30 DAYS



Learn in production order, not hype order
Use the chapters as a progression from one bounded agent to a durable, observable, controlled
system.

DAYS 1-7                  Chapters 01-05: loops, prompt contracts, tool schemas, tool execution, and
                          structured outputs.

DAYS 8-14                 Chapters 06-12: retries, resilience, cost control, state, memory, retrieval, and
                          planning.

DAYS 15-21                Chapters 13-19: coordination, human approval, safety, identity, observability,
                          evaluation, and testing.

DAYS 22-30                Chapters 20-25: concurrency, durable workflows, idempotency, APIs, streaming
                          UX, and deployment operations.

                                                    0016
CHAPTER 01 / 40 SNIPPETS




Agent Loop Control
Bound work, preserve observations, and stop deliberately.


10 CORE PATTERNS / EACH APPLIED TO SUPPORT, RESEARCH, COMMERCE, OPERATIONS



01   Bounded agent loop                                    06   Step budget guard

02   Goal decomposition                                    07   Action-result pairing

03   Next-action selection                                 08   Scratch state separation

04   Observation injection                                 09   Finalization gate

05   Explicit stop condition                               10   Fallback response




                                                                                           0017
PATTERN 0001 / 1000                                                                                       CH 01   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Bounded agent loop | Support
Intent: apply bounded agent loop while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  state = {"goal": "resolve the billing question", "steps": 0, "observations": []}
  while state["steps"] < 8:
      action = model.next_action(state)
      if action.kind == "final":
          return action.value
      result = tools.call(action.name, action.args)
      state["observations"].append(result)
      state["steps"] += 1
  raise RuntimeError("agent step budget exhausted")

USE IT     Use whenever the model can take more than one step or tool action.

WATCH      A loop needs both a step ceiling and an explicit terminal state; one does not replace the other.

Goal decomposition | Support
Intent: apply goal decomposition while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  goal = "resolve the billing question"
  tasks = model.decompose(goal, max_tasks=5)
  results = []
  for task in tasks:
      results.append(agent.run(task, max_steps=4))
  return model.synthesize(goal, results)




USE IT     Use whenever the model can take more than one step or tool action.

WATCH      A loop needs both a step ceiling and an explicit terminal state; one does not replace the other.

PATTERN 0009 / 1000                                                                                       CH 01   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Next-action selection | Support
Intent: apply next-action selection while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  candidates = tools.allowed_for("support_agent")
  action = model.choose_action(goal="resolve the billing question", tools=candidates)
  if action.name not in {t.name for t in candidates}:
      raise PermissionError(action.name)
  return tools.call(action.name, action.args)




USE IT     Use whenever the model can take more than one step or tool action.

WATCH      A loop needs both a step ceiling and an explicit terminal state; one does not replace the other.

PATTERN 0013 / 1000                                                                                       CH 01   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Observation injection | Support
Intent: apply observation injection while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  history = [{"role": "user", "content": "resolve the billing question"}]
  call = model.next_action(history)
  result = tools.call(call.name, call.args)
  history.append({"role": "tool", "tool_call_id": call.id,
                  "content": json.dumps(result)})
  return model.next_action(history)




USE IT     Use whenever the model can take more than one step or tool action.

WATCH      A loop needs both a step ceiling and an explicit terminal state; one does not replace the other.

PATTERN 0017 / 1000                                                                                       CH 01   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Explicit stop condition | Support
Intent: apply explicit stop condition while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  state = {"goal": "resolve the billing question", "done": False}
  for _ in range(8):
      state = agent.step(state)
      if state["done"]:
          return state["answer"]
  raise RuntimeError("no explicit stop condition reached")




USE IT     Use whenever the model can take more than one step or tool action.

WATCH      A loop needs both a step ceiling and an explicit terminal state; one does not replace the other.

PATTERN 0021 / 1000                                                                                       CH 01   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Step budget guard | Support
Intent: apply step budget guard while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  remaining = 6
  state = {"goal": "resolve the billing question"}
  while remaining:
      state = agent.step(state)
      if state.get("final"):
          return state["final"]
      remaining -= 1
  return {"status": "needs_review", "reason": "step_budget"}

USE IT     Use whenever the model can take more than one step or tool action.

WATCH      A loop needs both a step ceiling and an explicit terminal state; one does not replace the other.




                                                                                                                                      0038

  # Operations scenario: triage the production alert
  remaining = 6
  state = {"goal": "triage the production alert"}
  while remaining:
      state = agent.step(state)
      if state.get("final"):
          return state["final"]
      remaining -= 1
  return {"status": "needs_review", "reason": "step_budget"}

USE IT     Use whenever the model can take more than one step or tool action.

WATCH      A loop needs both a step ceiling and an explicit terminal state; one does not replace the other.

  if not all(checks):
      return {"status": "needs_review", "draft": draft}
  return {"status": "complete", "answer": draft}

Fallback response | Support

  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question

      answer = agent.run("resolve the billing question", max_steps=6)
      if answer:
          return answer
  except RecoverableAgentError as exc:
      trace("agent_fallback", error=type(exc).__name__)
  return "I could not complete this safely; a human should review it."

CHAPTER 03 / 40 SNIPPETS




Tool Schemas
Constrain tool arguments before a model can execute them.


10 CORE PATTERNS / EACH APPLIED TO SUPPORT, RESEARCH, COMMERCE, OPERATIONS



01   Object schema                                         06   Numeric bounds

02   Enum argument                                         07   String length bounds

03   Required fields                                       08   Nested object

04   Default normalization                                 09   Tagged action union

05   Reject unknown fields                                 10   Schema version




                                                                                       0099
PATTERN 0081 / 1000                                                                                       CH 03   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Object schema | Support
Intent: apply object schema while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  schema = {"type": "object", "properties": {
      "ticket_id": {"type": "string"},
      "query": {"type": "string"}},
      "required": ["ticket_id", "query"], "additionalProperties": False}
  tools.register("account_lookup", schema=schema, handler=lookup)




USE IT     Use before exposing a callable capability to model-generated arguments.

WATCH      Validate both shape and semantics; reject unknown fields instead of silently accepting them.

Numeric bounds | Support
Intent: apply numeric bounds while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  limit = args.get("limit", 10)
  if isinstance(limit, bool) or not isinstance(limit, int):
      raise TypeError("limit must be an integer")
  if not 1 <= limit <= 50:
      raise ValueError("limit must be 1..50")
  return lookup(limit=limit)

  return lookup(limit=limit)

Tool registry | Support
Intent: apply tool registry while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  REGISTRY = {"account_lookup": lookup, "summarize": summarize}
  def call_tool(name, args):
      handler = REGISTRY.get(name)
      if handler is None:
          raise KeyError(f"unknown tool: {name}")
      return handler(**args)




USE IT     Use at the boundary where an agent turns intent into executable code.

WATCH      Keep side effects idempotent and normalize tool errors into a stable agent-facing contract.

Safe parallel calls | Support
Intent: apply safe parallel calls while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  independent = [c for c in calls if c.read_only]
  if len(independent) != len(calls):
      raise ValueError("only read-only tools may run in parallel")
  results = await asyncio.gather(
      *(tools.call_async(c.name, c.args) for c in independent))
  return results

      raise ValueError("model returned invalid JSON") from exc
  return data

  if not isinstance(data.get("answer"), str):
      raise TypeError("answer must be text")
  return {"status": data["status"], "answer": data["answer"]}

Field coercion | Support
Intent: apply field coercion while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  data = json.loads(raw)
  value = data.get("confidence")
  if isinstance(value, str) and value.replace(".", "", 1).isdigit():
      value = float(value)
  if not isinstance(value, (int, float)) or isinstance(value, bool):
      raise TypeError("confidence must be numeric")
  data["confidence"] = float(value)
  return data

Discriminated result | Support
Intent: apply discriminated result while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  data = json.loads(raw)
  kind = data.get("kind")
  if kind == "answer" and isinstance(data.get("text"), str):
      return data
  if kind == "tool" and isinstance(data.get("name"), str):
      return data
  raise ValueError("invalid discriminated result")



USE IT     Use whenever downstream code depends on model output fields or types.

CHAPTER 06 / 40 SNIPPETS




Retry and Backoff
Retry transient failures without multiplying side effects.


10 CORE PATTERNS / EACH APPLIED TO SUPPORT, RESEARCH, COMMERCE, OPERATIONS



01   Fixed delay retry                                     06   Idempotent retry

02   Exponential backoff                                   07   Attempt cap

03   Full jitter                                           08   Deadline-aware retry

04   Retry-After handling                                  09   Retry budget

05   Selective retry                                       10   Failure escalation




                                                                                       0222
PATTERN 0201 / 1000                                                                                    CH 06   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Fixed delay retry | Support
Intent: apply fixed delay retry while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  for attempt in range(3):
      try:
          return tools.call("account_lookup", args)
      except TransientError:
          if attempt == 2:
              raise
          time.sleep(1.0)



USE IT     Use only for errors that are transient and safe to repeat.

WATCH      Do not retry permission, validation, or permanent business errors. Jitter shared retries.

Exponential backoff | Support
Intent: apply exponential backoff while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  delay = 0.5
  for attempt in range(4):
      try:
          return tools.call("account_lookup", args)
      except TransientError:
          if attempt == 3: raise
          time.sleep(delay)
          delay = min(delay * 2, 8.0)

USE IT     Use only for errors that are transient and safe to repeat.

Full jitter | Support
Intent: apply full jitter while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  base = 0.5
  for attempt in range(5):
      try:
          return tools.call("account_lookup", args)
      except TransientError:
          if attempt == 4: raise
          cap = min(8.0, base * (2 ** attempt))
          time.sleep(random.uniform(0, cap))

USE IT     Use only for errors that are transient and safe to repeat.

WATCH      Do not retry permission, validation, or permanent business errors. Jitter shared retries.

Retry-After handling | Support
Intent: apply retry-after handling while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  response = http_call()
  if response.status == 429:
      delay = min(float(response.headers.get("Retry-After", "1")), 30.0)
      time.sleep(delay)
      response = http_call()
  response.raise_for_status()
  return response



USE IT     Use only for errors that are transient and safe to repeat.

WATCH      Do not retry permission, validation, or permanent business errors. Jitter shared retries.

Selective retry | Support
Intent: apply selective retry while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  RETRYABLE = (TimeoutError, ConnectionError)
  for attempt in range(3):
      try:
          return tools.call("account_lookup", args)
      except RETRYABLE:
          if attempt == 2: raise
      except PermissionError:
          raise # never retry authorization failures

USE IT     Use only for errors that are transient and safe to repeat.

WATCH      Do not retry permission, validation, or permanent business errors. Jitter shared retries.

          return tools.call("account_lookup", args)
      except TransientError:
          trace("retry", attempt=attempt, max=max_attempts)
          if attempt >= max_attempts: raise



USE IT     Use only for errors that are transient and safe to repeat.

WATCH      Do not retry permission, validation, or permanent business errors. Jitter shared retries.

Retry budget | Support
Intent: apply retry budget while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  budget = retry_budgets.for_key(f"{request.tenant_id}:account_lookup",
                                 max_retries=12, window_seconds=60)
  try:
      return tools.call("account_lookup", args)
  except TransientError:
      if not budget.take():
          raise RuntimeError("shared retry budget exhausted")
      return tools.call("account_lookup", args)

USE IT     Use only for errors that are transient and safe to repeat.

CHAPTER 07 / 40 SNIPPETS




Timeouts and Resilience
Contain slow or unhealthy dependencies before they consume the agent.


10 CORE PATTERNS / EACH APPLIED TO SUPPORT, RESEARCH, COMMERCE, OPERATIONS



01   Per-call timeout                                      06   Stale-cache fallback

02   Global deadline                                       07   Watchdog timer

03   Circuit breaker                                       08   Cancel propagation

04   Bulkhead isolation                                    09   Dependency health gate

05   Fallback path                                         10   Fail fast




                                                                                         0263
PATTERN 0241 / 1000                                                                                CH 07   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Per-call timeout | Support
Intent: apply per-call timeout while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  async def lookup_with_timeout(args):
      async with asyncio.timeout(3.0):
          return await tools.call_async("account_lookup", args)

  result = await lookup_with_timeout(call.args)
  return result




USE IT     Use around dependencies whose slowness can consume the agent's full budget.

WATCH      Timeouts must cancel real work where possible; otherwise abandoned work can keep consuming capacity.

Stale-cache fallback | Support
Intent: apply stale-cache fallback while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  cached = cache.get(request.ticket_id)
  try:
      fresh = tools.call("account_lookup", args, timeout=2.0)
      cache.put(request.ticket_id, fresh, ttl=300)
      return fresh
  except TimeoutError:
      return {**cached, "stale": True} if cached else None



USE IT     Use around dependencies whose slowness can consume the agent's full budget.

WATCH      Timeouts must cancel real work where possible; otherwise abandoned work can keep consuming capacity.

Stale-cache fallback | Ops
Intent: apply stale-cache fallback while the agent works to triage the production alert.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Operations scenario: triage the production alert

  try:
      fresh = tools.call("metrics_query", args, timeout=2.0)
      cache.put(request.incident_id, fresh, ttl=300)
      return fresh
  except TimeoutError:
      return {**cached, "stale": True} if cached else None

Adaptive concurrency | Support
Intent: apply adaptive concurrency while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  limit = concurrency.current("model")
  if metrics.p95("model_latency") > 2.5:
      limit = max(2, limit - 1)
  elif metrics.p95("model_latency") < 1.0:
      limit = min(16, limit + 1)
  concurrency.set("model", limit)




USE IT     Use before autonomy can create unbounded model, tool, or queue consumption.

WATCH      Treat cost and concurrency ceilings as runtime invariants, not prompt suggestions.

Immutable step state | Support
Intent: apply immutable step state while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  @dataclass(frozen=True)
  class AgentState:
      step: int
      status: str

  state = AgentState(step=0, status="running")
  next_state = dataclasses.replace(state, step=state.step + 1)

Finite state machine | Support
Intent: apply finite state machine while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  ALLOWED = {"queued": {"running"}, "running": {"waiting", "done"},
             "waiting": {"running", "done"}, "done": set()}
  def transition(state, target):
      if target not in ALLOWED[state]:
          raise ValueError(f"illegal transition {state}->{target}")
      return target

  current, version = store.get_with_version(request.ticket_id)

Diversity selection | Support
Intent: apply diversity selection while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  candidates = retriever.search(request.text, top_k=20)
  selected = []
  for doc in candidates:
      if all(cosine(doc.embedding, x.embedding) < 0.92 for x in selected):
          selected.append(doc)
      if len(selected) == 8: break
  return selected

PATTERN 0421 / 1000                                                                                 CH 11   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Rerank stage | Support
Intent: apply rerank stage while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  candidates = retriever.search(request.text, top_k=20)
  pairs = [(request.text, doc.text) for doc in candidates]
  scores = reranker.score(pairs)
  ranked = sorted(zip(candidates, scores), key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
  return [doc for doc, score in ranked[:6] if score >= 0.35]




USE IT     Use when answers depend on external evidence rather than model memory alone.

WATCH      Preserve evidence IDs through ranking and abstain when the retrieved evidence is weak.

Chunk overlap | Support

  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  def chunks(text, size=1200, overlap=160):
      start = 0
      while start < len(text):
          yield text[start:start + size]
          start += size - overlap

  return list(chunks(document.text))

Task decomposition | Support
Intent: apply task decomposition while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  goal = "resolve the billing question"
  tasks = model.plan(goal, max_tasks=6)
  if len(tasks) > 6 or any(not t.success_criteria for t in tasks):
      raise ValueError("invalid task decomposition")
  return tasks




USE IT     Use when a goal requires several ordered, constrained, inspectable tasks.

Dependency graph | Support
Intent: apply dependency graph while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  deps = {"collect": set(), "verify": {"collect"}, "answer": {"verify"}}
  done = set()
  while len(done) < len(deps):
      ready = [t for t, need in deps.items() if t not in done and need <= done]
      if not ready: raise ValueError("cyclic task graph")
      done.add(run(ready[0]))




USE IT     Use when a goal requires several ordered, constrained, inspectable tasks.

WATCH      Replanning should preserve completed work and re-check permissions, budgets, and dependencies.

Priority queue | Support
Intent: apply priority queue while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  import heapq
  queue = []
  for task in tasks:
      heapq.heappush(queue, (task.priority, task.id, task))
  while queue:
      _, _, task = heapq.heappop(queue)
      run(task)

Replanning trigger | Support
Intent: apply replanning trigger while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  plan = model.plan("resolve the billing question")
  for task in plan:
      result = run(task)
      if result.invalidates_plan:
          plan = model.replan(goal="resolve the billing question", facts=result.facts,
                              completed=[t.id for t in plan if t.done])
          break

Plan approval | Support
Intent: apply plan approval while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  plan = model.plan("resolve the billing question", max_tasks=6)
  preview = [{"task": t.text, "tool": t.tool} for t in plan]
  if any(t.has_side_effect for t in plan):
      return {"status": "awaiting_approval", "plan": preview}
  return execute_plan(plan)

  # Operations scenario: triage the production alert
  plan = model.plan("triage the production alert", max_tasks=6)
  preview = [{"task": t.text, "tool": t.tool} for t in plan]
  if any(t.has_side_effect for t in plan):
      return {"status": "awaiting_approval", "plan": preview}
  return execute_plan(plan)

Milestone gate | Support

  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question

      results = [run(task) for task in milestone.tasks]
      if not all(r.ok for r in results):
          return {"status": "blocked", "milestone": milestone.name}
      checkpoint.save(milestone.name, results)
  return finalize()

Critical path | Support
Intent: apply critical path while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  duration = {"collect": 3, "verify": 2, "answer": 1}
  deps = {"collect": [], "verify": ["collect"], "answer": ["verify"]}
  finish = {}
  for task in topological_order(deps):
      start = max((finish[d] for d in deps[task]), default=0)
      finish[task] = start + duration[task]
  return max(finish.values())

Plan diff | Support
Intent: apply plan diff while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question

  new = {t.id: t for t in revised_plan}
  diff = {"added": sorted(new.keys() - old.keys()),
          "removed": sorted(old.keys() - new.keys()),
          "changed": sorted(k for k in old.keys() & new.keys() if old[k] != new[k])}
  return diff

Supervisor routing | Support
Intent: apply supervisor routing while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  workers = {"retrieve": retrieval_worker, "analyze": analysis_worker,
             "verify": verifier}
  route = supervisor.choose(request, workers=workers)
  if route not in {"retrieve", "analyze", "verify"}:
      raise ValueError("invalid worker route")
  return workers[route].run(request)




USE IT     Use only when specialization or parallelism adds value beyond one agent.

WATCH      More agents can add latency and disagreement. Bound rounds, isolate state, and validate handoffs.

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question

Isolated worker state | Support
Intent: apply isolated worker state while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  base = {"goal": "resolve the billing question", "tenant": request.tenant_id}
  worker_states = [copy.deepcopy(base) for _ in range(3)]
  results = await asyncio.gather(*(
      worker.run(state) for worker, state in zip(workers, worker_states)))
  return results

      audit("policy_block", actor="support_agent", action="issue_refund")

  return tools.call("issue_refund", args)

PII redaction | Support
Intent: apply pii redaction while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  EMAIL = re.compile(r"(?i)\b[A-Z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Z0-9.-]+\.[A-Z]{2,}\b")
  PHONE = re.compile(r"(?<!\d)(?:\+?\d[ -]?){7,15}(?!\d)")
  def redact_pii(text):
      text = EMAIL.sub("[EMAIL]", text)
      return PHONE.sub("[PHONE]", text)

  safe_text = redact_pii(tool_result)

Scoped token | Support
Intent: apply scoped token while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  token = auth.issue_token(subject=request.user_id,
                           scopes={"customer account:read"}, ttl=300)
  try:
      return tools.call("account_lookup", args, token=token)
  finally:
      auth.revoke(token)




USE IT     Use whenever an action must be attributable to a real principal and scope.

WATCH      Scope credentials narrowly, expire them quickly, and isolate tenants in both identity and storage keys.

  if identity.environment != ENVIRONMENT:
      raise PermissionError("service identity environment mismatch")
  return tools.call("metrics_query", args, identity=identity)

CHAPTER 17 / 40 SNIPPETS




Observability
Make each autonomous step traceable without leaking sensitive data.


10 CORE PATTERNS / EACH APPLIED TO SUPPORT, RESEARCH, COMMERCE, OPERATIONS



01   Correlation ID                                        06   Token metric

02   Trace span                                            07   Error taxonomy

03   Structured log                                        08   Trace sampling

04   Latency metric                                        09   Redacted trace

05   Tool metric                                           10   Replay record

Correlation ID | Support
Intent: apply correlation id while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  correlation_id = request.headers.get("X-Request-ID") or uuid.uuid4().hex
  with trace_context(correlation_id=correlation_id):
      result = agent.run("resolve the billing question")
  return {"request_id": correlation_id, "result": result}




USE IT     Use on every production run so failures can be diagnosed without replaying harm.

WATCH      Redact before telemetry leaves the process and keep high-cardinality or secret fields out of metric labels.

      if isinstance(exc, TimeoutError): return "dependency_timeout"
      if isinstance(exc, PermissionError): return "policy_denied"
      if isinstance(exc, ValueError): return "validation_error"
      return "internal_error"

  metrics.counter("agent_errors_total", 1, tags={"type": classify_error(exc)})

Exact-match scorer | Support
Intent: apply exact-match scorer while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  def exact_match(actual, expected):
      normalize = lambda s: " ".join(s.lower().split())
      return int(normalize(actual) == normalize(expected))

  score = exact_match(agent.run(test_input), expected_answer)
  assert score == 1

Pairwise preference | Support
Intent: apply pairwise preference while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  wins = 0
  for case in suite:
      pair = [("old", old_agent.run(case)), ("new", new_agent.run(case))]
      random.shuffle(pair)
      choice = judge.prefer(pair[0][1], pair[1][1],
                            criteria="grounded, correct, concise")
      winner = pair[0][0] if choice == "left" else pair[1][0]
      wins += int(winner == "new")
  return wins / len(suite)

USE IT     Use before releases and on every behavior change that could shift outcomes.

WATCH      Freeze the dataset and evaluator versions so a score change can be explained and reproduced.

PATTERN 0721 / 1000                                                                                  CH 19   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Fake model | Support
Intent: apply fake model while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  class FakeModel:
      def __init__(self, replies): self.replies = iter(replies)
      def complete(self, _prompt): return next(self.replies)

  agent = Agent(model=FakeModel(['{"status":"ok","answer":"done"}']))
  assert agent.run("resolve the billing question")["status"] == "ok"




USE IT     Use to prove retry, timeout, isolation, and policy behavior deterministically.

WATCH      Fakes should model the failure path you care about, not accidentally make every dependency perfect.

Fake tool | Support

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question

  def fake_tool(**args):

  agent.run("resolve the billing question", tools=tools)
  assert len(calls) == 1

CHAPTER 20 / 40 SNIPPETS




Async and Concurrency
Run independent work concurrently without losing bounds or ordering.


10 CORE PATTERNS / EACH APPLIED TO SUPPORT, RESEARCH, COMMERCE, OPERATIONS



01   Concurrent gather                                     06   Async timeout

02   Task group                                            07   Cooperative cancel

03   Semaphore bound                                       08   Backpressure

04   Queue worker                                          09   Ordered results

05   Producer consumer                                     10   Race-safe state




                                                                                     0796
PATTERN 0761 / 1000                                                                                CH 20   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Concurrent gather | Support
Intent: apply concurrent gather while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  async def collect(parts):
      tasks = [retrieve(part) for part in parts]
      results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
      return [r for r in results if r is not None]

  evidence = await collect(queries[:6])




USE IT     Use when work is independent enough to parallelize and bounded enough to control.

WATCH      Concurrency without backpressure only moves overload downstream. Bound queues, tasks, and cancellation.

  async with asyncio.TaskGroup() as group:
      tasks = [group.create_task(worker.run_async(item)) for item in items[:6]]
  for task in tasks:
      results.append(task.result())
  return results




USE IT     Use when work is independent enough to parallelize and bounded enough to control.

WATCH      Concurrency without backpressure only moves overload downstream. Bound queues, tasks, and cancellation.

Producer consumer | Support
Intent: apply producer consumer while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  queue = asyncio.Queue(maxsize=25)
  async def producer(items):
      for item in items:
          await queue.put(item) # producer slows when consumer is full
      await queue.put(None)

  async def consumer():
      while (item := await queue.get()) is not None: await process(item)

CHAPTER 21 / 40 SNIPPETS




Durable Workflows
Separate deterministic orchestration from retryable side effects.


10 CORE PATTERNS / EACH APPLIED TO SUPPORT, RESEARCH, COMMERCE, OPERATIONS



01   Workflow-activity split                               06   Workflow query

02   Deterministic workflow                                07   Durable timer

03   Activity retry                                        08   Compensation step

04   Heartbeat progress                                    09   Saga rollback

05   Workflow signal                                       10   History rollover




                                                                                    0837
PATTERN 0801 / 1000                                                                                      CH 21   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Workflow-activity split | Support
Intent: apply workflow-activity split while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  async def workflow(request):
      # Orchestration stays deterministic; I/O lives in an activity.
      record = await workflow_api.activity("account_lookup", request.ticket_id,
                                           timeout=10, retries=3)
      return await workflow_api.activity("finalize", record, timeout=20)




USE IT     Use when an agent must survive worker restarts, long waits, or external signals.

WATCH      Durable orchestration must be deterministic; put network I/O, randomness, and side effects in activities.

Deterministic workflow | Support
Intent: apply deterministic workflow while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  async def workflow(state):
      # Do not read wall-clock time or random directly inside durable logic.
      now = workflow_api.now()
      choice = workflow_api.side_effect("stable_choice", choose_once)
      state.update({"started_at": now, "choice": choice})
      return state

CHAPTER 22 / 40 SNIPPETS




Persistence and Idempotency
Persist effects so retries and replays remain safe.


10 CORE PATTERNS / EACH APPLIED TO SUPPORT, RESEARCH, COMMERCE, OPERATIONS



01   Dedupe key                                            06   Advisory lock

02   Transactional outbox                                  07   Optimistic version

03   Inbox dedupe                                          08   Once-only effect guard

04   Idempotent upsert                                     09   Replay-safe write

05   Lease ownership                                       10   Schema migration




                                                                                         0878
PATTERN 0841 / 1000                                                                                          CH 22   |   SUPPORT   |   PYTHON



Dedupe key | Support
Intent: apply dedupe key while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  fingerprint = hashlib.sha256(
      f"{request.tenant_id}:{request.id}:issue_refund".encode()).hexdigest()
  if previous := store.get(f"dedupe:{fingerprint}"):
      return previous
  result = tools.call("issue_refund", args, idempotency_key=fingerprint)
  store.put(f"dedupe:{fingerprint}", result, ttl=86400)
  return result



USE IT     Use whenever retries or duplicate deliveries could repeat a persistent effect.

WATCH      Exactly-once delivery is rarely available end to end; design effects to tolerate replay explicitly.

Replay-safe write | Support

  PYTHON 3.12

  result = persist_step(request, step_id, idempotency_key=event_id)

Request ID | Support
Intent: apply request id while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  request_id = headers.get("X-Request-ID") or uuid.uuid4().hex
  with trace_context(request_id=request_id):
      result = agent.run(payload)

  has_more = len(rows) > limit
  items = rows[:limit]
  next_cursor = encode_cursor(items[-1].id) if has_more else None
  return {"items": items, "next_cursor": next_cursor}

  PYTHON 3.12

             "RateLimit-Reset": str(int(reset_at))}
  if remaining <= 0:
      return 429, {**headers, "Retry-After": "2"}, {"error": "rate_limited"}
  return 200, headers, result

ETag caching | Support
Intent: apply etag caching while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  body = json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True).encode()
  etag = '"' + hashlib.sha256(body).hexdigest()[:24] + '"'
  if request.headers.get("If-None-Match") == etag:
      return 304, {"ETag": etag}, b""
  return 200, {"ETag": etag, "Cache-Control": "private, max-age=30"}, body

ETag caching | Research
Intent: apply etag caching while the agent works to synthesize the cited evidence.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Research scenario: synthesize the cited evidence
  body = json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True).encode()
  etag = '"' + hashlib.sha256(body).hexdigest()[:24] + '"'
  if request.headers.get("If-None-Match") == etag:
      return 304, {"ETag": etag}, b""
  return 200, {"ETag": etag, "Cache-Control": "private, max-age=30"}, body

Stream heartbeat | Support
Intent: apply stream heartbeat while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  async def with_heartbeat(source, every=10):
      last = time.monotonic()
      async for event in source:
          if time.monotonic() - last >= every:
              yield {"type": "heartbeat"}; last = time.monotonic()
          yield event

  async for event in with_heartbeat(agent_stream()): yield event

USE IT     Use when users need partial progress before the final, authoritative result.

WATCH      A streamed draft is provisional. Reconcile it with the final validated result and support cancellation.

Chunk buffering | Support

  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  buffer = []

      buffer.append(token)
      if len(buffer) >= 24 or token.endswith((". ", "? ", "! ")):
          yield "".join(buffer)
          buffer.clear()
  if buffer: yield "".join(buffer)

Partial JSON | Support
Intent: apply partial json while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  decoder = json.JSONDecoder()
  buffer = ""
  async for chunk in model.stream(prompt):
      buffer += chunk
      try:
          value, end = decoder.raw_decode(buffer)
          if buffer[end:].strip() == "": return validate(value)
      except json.JSONDecodeError:
          continue
  raise ValueError("stream ended before valid JSON")


USE IT     Use when users need partial progress before the final, authoritative result.

WATCH      A streamed draft is provisional. Reconcile it with the final validated result and support cancellation.

Tool progress | Support

  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question

      elif event.type == "tool_end":
          yield {"type": "progress_done", "id": event.id}
      else:
          yield serialize(event)

Final reconciliation | Support
Intent: apply final reconciliation while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  streamed = []
  async for event in agent.events(request):
      if event.type == "token": streamed.append(event.text)
      yield event
  final = await agent.final_result(request.id)
  if "".join(streamed) != final.text:
      yield {"type": "replace", "text": final.text}
  yield {"type": "done", "result_id": final.id}

Environment validation | Support
Intent: apply environment validation while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  required = ["MODEL_ENDPOINT", "STATE_STORE_URL", "ENVIRONMENT"]
  missing = [name for name in required if not os.getenv(name)]
  if missing:
      raise RuntimeError(f"missing required environment: {', '.join(missing)}")
  if os.environ["ENVIRONMENT"] not in {"staging", "production"}:
      raise RuntimeError("invalid environment")

Intent: apply canary routing while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  bucket = int(hashlib.sha256(request.tenant_id.encode()).hexdigest()[:8], 16) % 100

  target = canary_agent if use_canary else stable_agent
  metrics.counter("agent_route", 1, tags={"canary": str(use_canary).lower()})

Intent: apply readiness probe while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  def readiness():
      checks = {"state_store": state_store.ping(timeout=0.2),
                "model_gateway": model_gateway.ready(timeout=0.2)}
      ok = all(checks.values())
      return (200 if ok else 503), {"ready": ok, "checks": checks}

  status, body = readiness()

Queue drain | Support
Intent: apply queue drain while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  server.stop_accepting()
  deadline = time.monotonic() + 20
  while queue.inflight() and time.monotonic() < deadline:
      await queue.wait_for_progress(timeout=1)
  if queue.inflight():
      await queue.requeue_owned(worker_id)
  return {"drained": queue.inflight() == 0}



USE IT     Use for every environment that must start, roll forward, drain, and roll back safely.

WATCH      Health checks, canaries, error budgets, and rollback switches are part of the agent feature, not extras.

Queue drain | Ops
Intent: apply queue drain while the agent works to triage the production alert.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Operations scenario: triage the production alert
  server.stop_accepting()
  deadline = time.monotonic() + 20
  while queue.inflight() and time.monotonic() < deadline:
      await queue.wait_for_progress(timeout=1)
  if queue.inflight():
      await queue.requeue_owned(worker_id)
  return {"drained": queue.inflight() == 0}



USE IT     Use for every environment that must start, roll forward, drain, and roll back safely.

WATCH      Health checks, canaries, error budgets, and rollback switches are part of the agent feature, not extras.

SLO error budget | Support
Intent: apply slo error budget while the agent works to resolve the billing question.


  PYTHON 3.12

  # Support scenario: resolve the billing question
  target_success = 0.995
  observed_success = metrics.ratio("agent_success", window="30d")
  total_budget = 1 - target_success
  consumed = max(0.0, 1 - observed_success)
  burn = consumed / total_budget if total_budget else float("inf")
  if burn > 2: flags.freeze_rollouts("agent")
  return {"error_budget_burn": burn}

CLOSING / PRODUCTION STANDARD




Make autonomy boring on purpose
A strong agent is powerful because its boundaries are obvious.


01    Bound every loop, timeout, retry, queue, and cost.

02    Validate every tool argument and structured model output.

03    Require explicit permission and approval before high-impact effects.

04    Persist state and effects so retries, replays, and resumes are safe.

05    Test failures, trace decisions, evaluate releases, and keep rollback cheap.
